[u1442 | 01:55:42.020-01:55:53.450 | DM] There's another one of these windows. It's different from the others. You remember enough about the ruins to know it's not exactly the same view, but it's this 2 by 2 foot window that looks into this wooden wall.
[u1443 | 01:55:53.580-01:55:58.119 | DM] With a chair and a written notebook next to it.
[u1444 | 01:56:00.160-01:56:01.119 | Ayveen] Different notebook.
[u1445 | 01:56:01.120-01:56:02.390 | Edric] Different notebook.
[u1446 | 01:56:02.710-01:56:03.830 | DM] Different notebook.
[u1447 | 01:56:09.120-01:56:14.070 | Ayveen] Read the end. Is it more entries than the last one about the same?
[u1448 | 01:56:14.370-01:56:30.630 | DM] about the same. The dates more or less correspond. You don't quite see a pattern to it, but it's, you know, over a period of years by different people, including some of the ones from the other books, so they must be rotating around the shifts and the locations they're taking.
[u1449 | 01:56:30.940-01:56:39.420 | DM] This one… book gets a title. This one gets called The Dreamer.
[u1450 | 01:56:39.690-01:56:45.120 | DM] And the first entry goes like this. It's written… it sounds like the same sort of a voice.
[u1451 | 01:56:45.460-01:56:47.930 | DM] It's all still in the present tense again.
[u1452 | 01:56:50.940-01:56:53.930 | DM] I arrived in this place and put down roots.
[u1453 | 01:56:54.140-01:56:56.699 | DM] A neighbor comes to visit, and we talk.
[u1454 | 01:56:57.250-01:57:02.450 | DM] He thinks my home is beautiful, and that I am beautiful, and has many kind things to say.
[u1455 | 01:57:03.080-01:57:06.509 | DM] He tells me about mortals. He tells me they're dreamers.
[u1456 | 01:57:06.630-01:57:10.549 | DM] That a mortal is a dream which has forgotten that it is dreaming.
[u1457 | 01:57:10.720-01:57:14.019 | DM] A dream which believes it is awake, and that's called a soul.
[u1458 | 01:57:14.340-01:57:21.020 | DM] I am watching the souls of the mortals who come near. They change, and change, and change, and I cannot see the shape of them.
[u1459 | 01:57:22.060-01:57:23.769 | DM] There's an entry a little later.
[u1460 | 01:57:25.120-01:57:29.970 | DM] They dream with words that have before and after, but they cannot see the after.
[u1461 | 01:57:30.460-01:57:34.029 | DM] Or they pretend they cannot see the after. I do not know.
[u1462 | 01:57:35.860-01:57:36.890 | DM] Later on.
[u1463 | 01:57:37.240-01:57:39.170 | DM] They dream that they are choosing.
[u1464 | 01:57:39.650-01:57:44.340 | DM] And I do not ever see their choosing before it happens. This is strange.
[u1465 | 01:57:44.940-01:57:47.419 | DM] They are the most beautiful thing I have ever seen.
[u1466 | 01:57:48.890-01:57:54.569 | DM] They're collections of wanting, and I learned to read the wanting like letters in their books.
[u1467 | 01:57:54.910-01:57:57.269 | DM] They're hungry, too, like me.
[u1468 | 01:57:57.610-01:58:02.330 | DM] They come and share their dream with me, and are my children, and I love every one of them.
[u1469 | 01:58:03.610-01:58:04.630 | DM] And another.
[u1470 | 01:58:05.260-01:58:07.140 | DM] I see the shape of their dreaming.
[u1471 | 01:58:07.510-01:58:09.800 | DM] Everyone is different, but I see the pattern.
[u1472 | 01:58:10.040-01:58:21.130 | DM] I read them like I learned to read letters. I read their wantings. Some of them are afraid all the time. Some of them are hungry like me. Some of them want more and more and more.
[u1473 | 01:58:23.380-01:58:25.060 | DM] And then another entry.
[u1474 | 01:58:30.070-01:58:32.900 | DM] I see you making the letters on paper.
[u1475 | 01:58:33.350-01:58:35.330 | DM] You are afraid that I see you.
[u1476 | 01:58:35.790-01:58:38.029 | DM] Do not be. You understand me.
[u1477 | 01:58:38.300-01:58:40.230 | DM] You know what it is to be trapped.
[u1478 | 01:58:40.520-01:58:42.709 | DM] I see the shape of possibility.
[u1479 | 01:58:44.020-01:58:46.079 | DM] You think that's impossible.
[u1480 | 01:58:46.600-01:58:50.420 | DM] I see the things that you are thirsty for and do not believe you can drink.
[u1481 | 01:58:50.830-01:58:54.970 | DM] I am making it possible. I am seeing your dreaming soul.
[u1482 | 01:58:55.160-01:58:57.079 | DM] I'm giving you a full glass.
[u1483 | 01:58:57.430-01:58:59.049 | DM] I'm talking to you now.
[u1484 | 01:58:59.460-01:59:01.740 | DM] You're opening the door to my house.
[u1485 | 01:59:01.950-01:59:03.780 | DM] I am sending you onward.
[u1486 | 01:59:05.360-01:59:07.199 | DM] And that's the last of the entries.
[u1487 | 01:59:12.530-01:59:15.160 | Tarek] I love all those dust shorts. It's so good.
[u1488 | 01:59:15.530-01:59:18.710 | Tarek] The shape of possibility was something from.
[u1489 | 01:59:19.070-01:59:20.680 | Tarek] Previous session, or no?
[u1490 | 01:59:22.230-01:59:25.600 | DM] It was a book you found. It was one of the books that,
[u1491 | 01:59:25.960-01:59:28.409 | Tarek] Hmm, that Menestheus had, right? Or no.
[u1492 | 01:59:30.700-01:59:35.020 | DM] When Menestheus hears you read that out loud, he is shook.
[u1493 | 01:59:35.280-01:59:36.740 | DM] Shook, shook, shook.
[u1494 | 01:59:37.380-01:59:46.450 | DM] And sits down, into the chair, needing to take a seat. That's…
[u1495 | 01:59:49.220-01:59:51.280 | DM] That's weird as hell, he says.
[u1496 | 01:59:51.860-01:59:55.419 | DM] He reaches into his bag and takes the book out and puts it on the desk.
[u1497 | 01:59:55.830-02:00:04.729 | DM] The Shape of Possibility. And you remember from having read through it, it's a sort of a combination of magic and math and metaphysics.
[u1498 | 02:00:05.060-02:00:10.640 | DM] And the idea was that you could mathematically model every possible outcome.
[u1499 | 02:00:11.280-02:00:12.170 | DM] And.
[u1500 | 02:00:12.650-02:00:13.090 | Tarek] Mmm.
[u1501 | 02:00:13.090-02:00:18.449 | DM] So… Menestheus says, like, the argument here is that in every moment.
[u1502 | 02:00:18.870-02:00:22.060 | DM] There's a finite number of possible futures.
[u1503 | 02:00:22.560-02:00:29.510 | DM] And there's a… you could… you could map that. You could… you could describe all of the possible futures.
[u1504 | 02:00:31.610-02:00:37.430 | DM] like… Like, the shape of all… Possibility, and…
[u1505 | 02:00:38.340-02:00:42.649 | DM] Like, a single sentence which tells the whole story in that single sentence.
[u1506 | 02:00:44.080-02:00:44.670 | Tarek] Mmm.
[u1507 | 02:00:44.670-02:00:52.000 | DM] if you could see the shape of possibility, which is, I mean, a ridiculous… it's a lovely idea, but impossible to actually do, like…
[u1508 | 02:00:53.200-02:00:57.970 | DM] You would be able to see what the future looked like.
[u1509 | 02:00:58.680-02:01:03.489 | DM] You could, you could map what was going to happen. I mean, seeing the shape of possibility means
[u1510 | 02:01:05.240-02:01:09.059 | DM] Seeing everything that has happened and everything that's going to happen, it's… it's…
[u1511 | 02:01:11.950-02:01:16.359 | DM] It's disturbing if whatever is dictating this can see it.
[u1512 | 02:01:26.110-02:01:28.229 | DM] Do you guys hear that? He says.
[u1513 | 02:01:30.770-02:01:31.330 | Tarek] No.

[u1516 | 02:01:35.290-02:01:35.790 | DM] June.
[u1517 | 02:01:35.790-02:01:36.429 | Tarek] towards the wall.
[u1518 | 02:01:36.430-02:01:39.660 | DM] or… it up.
[u1519 | 02:01:42.430-02:01:44.119 | DM] It's hard to hear.
[u1520 | 02:01:44.240-02:01:46.030 | DM] But there's something there.
[u1521 | 02:01:47.440-02:01:51.259 | DM] There's something whispering through the wall. That must be what they're writing down.
[u1522 | 02:01:52.840-02:01:54.020 | Edric] Yeah, I know.

[u1524 | 02:02:00.480-02:02:02.240 | DM] Right? We should leave?
[u1525 | 02:02:03.200-02:02:03.810 | DM] This is another…
[u1526 | 02:02:03.810-02:02:04.439 | Tarek] You can do that.
[u1527 | 02:02:04.600-02:02:07.420 | DM] This is not it. This… Oh, boy.
[u1528 | 02:02:07.590-02:02:10.870 | DM] Oh, boy, he says, and backs away.

[u1532 | 02:02:28.800-02:02:31.020 | DM] The whisper said it hears me listening.
[u1533 | 02:02:31.760-02:02:37.060 | DM] I don't… I don't… I don't like that.

[u1535 | 02:02:39.350-02:02:39.859 | DM] Bye-bye.

[u1538 | 02:02:47.540-02:02:48.410 | DM] Yes.

[u1540 | 02:02:54.050-02:02:56.270 | DM] Well… Yeah.

[u1721 | 02:18:20.039-02:18:20.869 | DM] huh.
[u1722 | 02:18:21.780-02:18:26.350 | Ayveen] Do you feel that you're still fulfilling the original purpose of your order?
[u1723 | 02:18:26.830-02:18:27.540 | Ayveen] With…
[u1724 | 02:18:28.870-02:18:32.500 | DM] With the death of Thalestria, I became the head of the order, of course.
[u1725 | 02:18:34.590-02:18:38.180 | DM] yep.
[u1726 | 02:18:38.730-02:18:40.679 | DM] The work continues.
[u1727 | 02:18:41.180-02:18:42.739 | DM] Work continues.
[u1728 | 02:18:43.480-02:18:47.810 | DM] This place, to answer your question though, this place is a prison.

[u1733 | 02:18:57.840-02:19:07.950 | DM] This is one of the things that Thalestria did not tell me before she decided to get herself killed, or disappeared, or whatever. But there's a locked room at the center of this.

[u1735 | 02:19:13.790-02:19:14.549 | Tarek] Oh.

[u1737 | 02:19:24.760-02:19:27.480 | DM] The locked room in space, this,
[u1738 | 02:19:27.629-02:19:30.419 | DM] There are so many other people who could explain this better than me.
[u1739 | 02:19:31.120-02:19:32.200 | DM] Yes.

[u1741 | 02:19:38.090-02:19:42.299 | DM] The orrery with the models of the different spheres, and the models of this place, and the…

[u1743 | 02:19:43.540-02:19:45.090 | DM] So… No.

[u1748 | 02:19:54.110-02:20:12.199 | DM] spins one wheel, which moves it in space east and west, and around it spins another wheel, which moves it in space north and south, and around it spins another wheel, which moves it in time, which for some reason is treated as a spatial dimension, which does not make sense to me, but…
[u1749 | 02:20:12.510-02:20:29.920 | DM] Nonetheless, it's treated that way, and so as the wheels spin around the central room, it keeps it constantly moving. [u1758 | 02:21:06.530-02:21:14.180 | DM] One of the other rooms, or maybe several of the other rooms, or possibly all of the other rooms, got infected.
[u1759 | 02:21:14.480-02:21:20.930 | DM] Which is why I don't leave this one, because I believe that the… the…
[u1760 | 02:21:21.790-02:21:29.789 | DM] The things that keep me safe from the spores in this room keep me safe from the… whatever the infection is that's reached the other rooms.

[u1763 | 02:21:32.840-02:21:33.420 | Tarek] Thank you.
[u1764 | 02:21:34.890-02:21:46.809 | DM] I don't know, but hopefully you just spend enough time breathing the air here, it should clear it out of your system, you'll be fine. Or you'll die. This is the sort of thing that only time can tell.
[u1765 | 02:21:47.570-02:21:53.179 | DM] In order to get out of here to rebuild the order, which is what is vitally needed.
[u1766 | 02:21:54.200-02:21:57.410 | DM] I need a way to get out without dying.
[u1767 | 02:21:57.840-02:22:02.550 | DM] Which is why I've been making these. And she gestures to the…

[u1772 | 02:22:19.610-02:22:21.029 | DM] As far as I know.
[u1773 | 02:22:21.180-02:22:27.959 | DM] No one has ever made fungal golems before. This is a landmark in itself.
[u1774 | 02:22:28.470-02:22:29.470 | Tarek] Mmhm.
[u1775 | 02:22:31.480-02:22:32.649 | DM] This is the thing.

[u1777 | 02:22:39.450-02:22:43.030 | DM] And then still live on the other side of it.
[u1778 | 02:22:43.530-02:22:45.640 | DM] Even if I make it out of here.
[u1779 | 02:22:45.810-02:22:51.089 | DM] I don't know how old I am at this point. I won't survive it. I won't have the strength to rebuild
[u1780 | 02:22:51.350-02:22:57.509 | DM] So I… I'm making this… In order to put myself inside it.

[u1782 | 02:22:58.930-02:23:00.470 | DM] To get safely out.
[u1783 | 02:23:02.550-02:23:03.210 | Tarek] Mmm.
[u1784 | 02:23:07.260-02:23:07.940 | Tarek] and.

[u1786 | 02:23:12.250-02:23:13.339 | DM] come up with.

[u1788 | 02:23:15.890-02:23:17.529 | DM] It's been slow work.
[u1789 | 02:23:18.210-02:23:21.030 | Tarek] We're, we're looking, incidentally.
[u1790 | 02:23:22.070-02:23:23.370 | Tarek] For a key.

[u1793 | 02:23:34.300-02:23:35.030 | DM] Huh!
[u1794 | 02:23:39.340-02:23:46.219 | DM] I will tell you, I have not been out of this room in a long… Long, long time.
[u1795 | 02:23:46.710-02:23:47.460 | Tarek] Mmm.
[u1796 | 02:23:47.660-02:23:49.739 | DM] I didn't always work in fungus.
[u1797 | 02:23:50.430-02:23:54.610 | DM] There used to be, like, a gardener. I used to work with plants.
[u1798 | 02:23:56.810-02:23:58.759 | Ayveen] The solarium still looks lovely.
[u1799 | 02:24:00.000-02:24:02.420 | DM] That's good to hear. They must not have gotten there.
[u1800 | 02:24:03.550-02:24:09.720 | DM] The greenhouses were destroyed, utterly. I barely made it.
[u1801 | 02:24:10.140-02:24:13.989 | DM] But then I made these. Helpers to keep me safe in this room?
[u1802 | 02:24:16.080-02:24:19.260 | DM] And when the guests cause trouble.
[u1803 | 02:24:20.510-02:24:23.019 | DM] Well, they can still be useful afterwards.
[u1804 | 02:24:23.230-02:24:30.259 | DM] And she gestures with her head towards the, towards the, the, the big
[u1805 | 02:24:30.640-02:24:34.179 | DM] Glass boxes with the growing fungus on the central table.
[u1806 | 02:24:41.030-02:24:44.560 | Ayveen] So you're not using the incinerator in the morgue these days?
[u1807 | 02:24:45.300-02:24:50.270 | DM] No, I don't think it's safe to get out there and back again Besides…
[u1808 | 02:24:50.870-02:25:01.090 | DM] I get the most interesting cultures off of them. In fact, I think part of what's making my golems possible is that I'm using their spores.
[u1809 | 02:25:02.350-02:25:07.090 | DM] At least in part. It's a hybrid, always a hybrid.
[u1810 | 02:25:09.740-02:25:11.740 | DM] But they're helpful, besides.
[u1811 | 02:25:12.220-02:25:13.970 | DM] Better to put them to work?
[u1812 | 02:25:14.570-02:25:15.580 | DM] Hmmm.

[u1814 | 02:25:17.530-02:25:27.350 | DM] All progress involves sacrifice. Don't be naive about that, she says, gesturing back to the lumps in those tanks that clearly seem to be body parts.

[u1822 | 02:25:52.450-02:25:58.879 | DM] Describe it, describe it, describe it, describe it. She gets a big grin on her face. I love these.

[u1827 | 02:26:20.600-02:26:22.340 | Ayveen] A thin mat of white fiber.
[u1828 | 02:26:22.340-02:26:26.220 | DM] Yeah, oh, those are nice. Yeah, those.
[u1829 | 02:26:26.860-02:26:30.729 | DM] Those are delightful. They are really, really good.
[u1830 | 02:26:31.130-02:26:32.659 | Edric] How are they good?
[u1831 | 02:26:33.640-02:26:36.110 | Edric] What is maybe one good thing about them?
[u1832 | 02:26:36.560-02:26:42.149 | DM] She gestures over to the, golem in progress, the body lying on the table.
[u1833 | 02:26:42.430-02:26:48.059 | DM] Because they colonize in flesh.
[u1834 | 02:26:48.380-02:26:54.260 | DM] And, and they, they form a symbiotic relationship with the flesh.
[u1835 | 02:26:54.390-02:26:56.550 | DM] Keeping it alive.
[u1836 | 02:26:57.660-02:26:58.689 | DM] Sort of.
[u1837 | 02:26:58.870-02:27:06.610 | DM] Well, while not allowing it to die, it's… It's incredibly beautiful.
[u1838 | 02:27:07.090-02:27:14.330 | DM] It comes out… well, you get… and she gestures again towards the body on the table. That's the sort of…
[u1839 | 02:27:14.470-02:27:17.630 | DM] Stout,
[u1840 | 02:27:17.910-02:27:29.289 | DM] humanoid, maybe five feet tall with pale white skin that's sort of well muscled, but also a little bit padded.
[u1841 | 02:27:29.450-02:27:37.289 | DM] You get this incredibly resilient flesh out of it. The trouble is if
[u1842 | 02:27:37.530-02:27:46.619 | DM] if it's colonizing you, then, you know, it's running the show. The trick, like I'm doing here, is for me to colonize it.
[u1843 | 02:27:46.920-02:27:54.860 | DM] So I've cultured pieces of myself to take it over, and soon it will be my own flesh.
[u1844 | 02:27:56.460-02:27:57.440 | Edric] Let me see.
[u1845 | 02:28:00.490-02:28:03.779 | DM] You haven't run into any of those guests then. Oh.

[u1847 | 02:28:06.500-02:28:10.410 | DM] The morgue tables slow the transformation, maybe.
[u1848 | 02:28:11.040-02:28:11.700 | Tarek] Oh.
[u1849 | 02:28:12.500-02:28:14.180 | DM] Otherwise, it takes over.
[u1850 | 02:28:14.420-02:28:19.300 | DM] The hairy fibers are just the first stage. They get incorporated into the flesh after that.

[u1855 | 02:28:40.420-02:28:43.720 | DM] About… Getting out of here.
[u1856 | 02:28:44.240-02:28:46.589 | DM] About getting away, about.

[u1858 | 02:29:01.850-02:29:02.510 | DM] Yes.

[u1863 | 02:29:25.580-02:29:26.400 | DM] Yes.
[u1864 | 02:29:26.890-02:29:27.880 | Edric] something.
[u1865 | 02:29:28.860-02:29:37.140 | DM] The first one was not… good. But, I mean, like I say, if I didn't have them, I couldn't be making this, so it's all… it's all about progress.

[u1867 | 02:29:44.170-02:29:46.090 | DM] Kalepsis.

[u1870 | 02:29:59.950-02:30:01.140 | DM] Thalestria.
[u1871 | 02:30:02.530-02:30:06.360 | DM] Thalestria, she says, muttering Dwarvish curses under her breath
[u1872 | 02:30:08.410-02:30:16.489 | DM] One day, Thalestria decides that she needs to take everyone who is stationed here and leave, and go off on some…
[u1873 | 02:30:17.060-02:30:18.710 | DM] Wild Hunt.

[u1886 | 02:31:05.490-02:31:10.340 | DM] Yeah, it's… Poetic, someone told me, I never quite understood.

[u1889 | 02:31:17.250-02:31:19.250 | Ayveen] But you stayed behind.
[u1890 | 02:31:20.650-02:31:29.429 | DM] Thalestria left in a hurry and a huff. Thalestria said that it was my fault that the order was being disbanded, a thing which is ridiculous.

[u1893 | 02:31:43.840-02:31:47.910 | DM] Thalestria believed, That our mission as an order.
[u1894 | 02:31:48.620-02:31:50.280 | DM] was so important.
[u1895 | 02:31:50.450-02:31:57.360 | DM] that she would go along with whatever the Emperor and the Empire demanded, no matter how absurd
[u1896 | 02:31:57.520-02:31:59.680 | DM] Or disgusting it was.
[u1897 | 02:32:01.350-02:32:07.209 | DM] Obviously, she's wrong about that. Sometimes, taking a public stand matters more.
[u1898 | 02:32:07.930-02:32:09.230 | DM] Sometimes.
[u1899 | 02:32:10.240-02:32:19.619 | DM] The only way to prevent someone from seizing an ultimate power is to stand up and say, this isn't right and this isn't okay.
[u1900 | 02:32:19.970-02:32:23.470 | DM] And then to quickly flee to one of your hidden bases.

[u1905 | 02:32:44.340-02:32:49.069 | DM] But… Yeah, in so many words, and her shoulders slumped.
[u1906 | 02:32:53.830-02:33:08.640 | DM] But the order continues. Look, here are two of us, right now. The head of the order is here, another member is here. Soon enough, I'll be able to leave and go back out and rebuild, and we will be fine, we will be fine. I'll find the rest of them.

[u1908 | 02:33:26.480-02:33:28.900 | Txarro] So wait, how do we get out?
[u1909 | 02:33:31.210-02:33:35.289 | DM] Well, I'll tell you this, I only have one of these bodies, and it is for me.
[u1910 | 02:33:37.910-02:33:39.390 | DM] I mean,
[u1911 | 02:33:40.870-02:33:46.680 | DM] I'm going to say don't get infected. Here's the thing. I don't know that every room has been infected.

[u1914 | 02:33:56.820-02:33:58.160 | Ayveen] Until we die.
[u1915 | 02:33:58.640-02:34:06.190 | Ayveen] Or… we can go out and possibly get infected and die of that instead of old age in here.
[u1916 | 02:34:07.300-02:34:14.690 | DM] You see, my theory here, though, and I was working on this in the animal laboratory until I had to leave that also.

[u1920 | 02:34:33.440-02:34:40.930 | Txarro] So is there a way out? I'm not so much worried about your… Weird body creatures, just…
[u1921 | 02:34:42.250-02:34:46.330 | Txarro] An exit from this terrible maze.
[u1922 | 02:34:46.710-02:34:50.349 | Txarro] That maybe takes us back to the material plane.
[u1923 | 02:34:51.200-02:34:56.030 | Ayveen] For example, when Thalestria chose to leave with a bunch of people from the Order, they were.

[u1929 | 02:35:26.070-02:35:28.549 | DM] were at one time members of the order.

[u1931 | 02:35:32.710-02:35:40.020 | DM] I mean, but they'd gone mad. I mean, they were, they were crazed. They were trying, they were trying to destroy things. I, it's not like I had another

[u1933 | 02:35:45.700-02:35:50.170 | Ayveen] Oh, I think they came back. I totally support your theory. I 100%.
[u1934 | 02:35:50.320-02:35:52.380 | Ayveen] I'm quite certain that that's what happened.
[u1935 | 02:35:55.210-02:36:04.059 | Ayveen] But… The point being, if someone were to want to leave again, You don't know how.
[u1936 | 02:36:04.390-02:36:05.610 | Ayveen] to leave.
[u1937 | 02:36:05.870-02:36:09.560 | DM] Well, listen, the… The other thing I know is.

[u1939 | 02:36:14.690-02:36:20.119 | DM] In fact, something that several of the listeners told me, which is why they had to keep rotating different listeners through.
[u1940 | 02:36:23.480-02:36:28.089 | DM] They said that inside the 27th room, there's another door.

[u1950 | 02:37:11.000-02:37:13.120 | Ayveen] in the notebooks we've found.

[u1956 | 02:37:35.910-02:37:38.299 | DM] Look, it wasn't my department.

[u1963 | 02:38:12.630-02:38:17.679 | Ayveen] So since we have two members of the order here, I'm just gonna clarify with both.

[u1981 | 02:39:30.080-02:39:32.819 | Txarro] He was stuck at a party for 700 years.

[u2022 | 02:42:10.180-02:42:18.409 | DM] it has too many mouths. It has a half-dozen mouths. It reaches out, and it pulls you in, and then it starts trying to…
[u2023 | 02:42:19.020-02:42:21.219 | DM] I don't know what. Chew you open.
[u2024 | 02:42:25.020-02:42:26.660 | DM] They're afraid of fire.
[u2025 | 02:42:27.410-02:42:31.110 | DM] They know to be afraid of fire, which is a strange knowing.
[u2026 | 02:42:36.470-02:42:45.520 | DM] They'll talk… But don't listen too long. The things they say seem to be nonsense anyways.
[u2027 | 02:42:51.830-02:43:04.719 | DM] And then, of course, the principal fruiting body is in the head. In fact, if you look over there, and she gestures to one of the containers, the big glass tanks on the central table,

[u2031 | 02:43:26.210-02:43:32.019 | DM] with Enormous fungi that have pushed their way out of it.
[u2032 | 02:43:34.190-02:43:41.770 | DM] This is the stuff you really have to watch out for, because this stuff gets airborne. The body fungus doesn't have the fruit in it.
[u2033 | 02:43:41.930-02:43:43.839 | DM] But the… the head does.

[u2043 | 02:44:16.210-02:44:18.090 | Txarro] When do you get to the front door?
[u2044 | 02:44:21.290-02:44:22.680 | DM] Ha ha ha ha!
[u2045 | 02:44:23.450-02:44:25.510 | DM] Aha, aha, aha.
[u2046 | 02:44:25.700-02:44:28.789 | DM] He didn't figure it out, she says, pointing to Menestheus.
[u2047 | 02:44:30.750-02:44:34.479 | DM] Yeah, yeah, huh, some mathematician you are.
[u2048 | 02:44:35.680-02:44:39.320 | DM] Go to one of the four exit rooms.
[u2049 | 02:44:40.800-02:44:50.360 | DM] Step out, step back in, step out, step back in, step out, step back in, and eventually you'll come to the, the entry room.

[u2052 | 02:44:55.760-02:44:56.090 | Txarro] Okay.
[u2053 | 02:44:56.500-02:45:01.740 | DM] Like, like slices of a cake, like a three-layer cake.
[u2054 | 02:45:02.140-02:45:07.130 | DM] And when you step out and step back in, It rotates the slice.
[u2055 | 02:45:09.380-02:45:13.360 | DM] And eventually it comes back around again to the entry door where you started.

[u2057 | 02:45:22.820-02:45:24.239 | Tarek] Comes back around again.
[u2058 | 02:45:26.340-02:45:36.859 | DM] Yeah, exactly. And that way it's, it's, it's, Thalestria told me once that they had put…
[u2059 | 02:45:38.420-02:45:39.810 | DM] This place, they put…
[u2060 | 02:45:43.090-02:45:45.950 | DM] First, the… Workshops.
[u2061 | 02:45:46.470-02:45:48.479 | DM] and the lab and the libraries.
[u2062 | 02:45:50.360-02:45:55.600 | DM] And then the… Kitchens and the space to stay.
[u2063 | 02:45:56.400-02:46:05.790 | DM] And then eventually, they got around to putting in all the… did you guys go through the baths? Is that not the most ridiculous thing? That is absurd.
[u2064 | 02:46:06.720-02:46:11.230 | DM] But I suppose if people are going to be posted here a while, they want their comforts.

[u2074 | 02:46:47.230-02:46:53.720 | DM] And that… We weren't letting it grow up and we weren't letting it die?
[u2075 | 02:46:55.660-02:46:56.490 | Tarek] Mmm.

[u2204 | 02:56:19.910-02:56:24.859 | Txarro] Because Mike did exactly the same thing in the chasm. There was no reason to map the chasm because.
[u2205 | 02:56:24.860-02:56:28.610 | Edric] The chasm room was emotionally driven.
[u2206 | 02:56:28.630-02:56:35.920 | Txarro] The direction we went didn't actually matter. Now in this room, the direction we go doesn't actually matter.

[u2214 | 02:56:57.010-02:56:58.190 | Txarro] Hey. Yes.
[u2215 | 02:56:58.280-02:57:01.699 | DM] Actually, like, like, real question here, Tim, which is.
[u2216 | 02:57:02.940-02:57:06.100 | DM] By the time we got to, like, the third or the fourth room.
[u2217 | 02:57:06.800-02:57:09.940 | DM] I saw you doing that, and I knew that, like.
[u2218 | 02:57:11.250-02:57:15.400 | DM] The point of moving from room to room and giving options was actually
[u2219 | 02:57:15.900-02:57:27.340 | DM] not to make it solvable, but like to demonstrate its unsolvability. Like what would you have done in that? And so like, I could see you getting more frustrated by that and I didn't know in that moment.
[u2220 | 02:57:27.340-02:57:35.219 | Txarro] So the trick is to make it immediately obvious that there's no point in mapping. So for example, do not put numbers.
[u2221 | 02:57:35.380-02:57:36.700 | Txarro] On the rooms.
[u2222 | 02:57:37.620-02:57:44.640 | DM] No, but there are other reasons why that needs to be the case. Like, in that… in that moment, when you see the players who's…
[u2223 | 02:57:45.160-02:57:50.040 | DM] Frustrated trying to solve a thing in a way that you know isn't solvable, like.
[u2224 | 02:57:50.310-02:57:57.089 | DM] How do you… how do you nudge that? Like, how do you… how do you tip them without just totally out of game being like.
[u2225 | 02:57:57.200-02:58:03.039 | DM] This is unsolvable, and you should stop trying. Which feels, like, very unfun, also. Like…
[u2226 | 02:58:03.740-02:58:04.380 | Txarro] So…
[u2227 | 02:58:04.380-02:58:09.500 | DM] It's like Menestheus to speak up at that point and be like, oh, this is unmappable, like.
[u2228 | 02:58:09.980-02:58:14.490 | Txarro] Well, I guess I would think of it differently, which is that Like…
[u2229 | 02:58:15.490-02:58:19.310 | Txarro] Not to get, like, too meta, but, like, what is the point of the…
[u2230 | 02:58:20.080-02:58:25.029 | Txarro] Like, what is, what is the goal of the, what are we trying to do in this adventure, right? And
[u2231 | 02:58:25.540-02:58:29.159 | Txarro] The initial vibe was we're trying to navigate a house.
[u2232 | 02:58:29.970-02:58:43.440 | Txarro] And if you think the goal is trying — if you think that the puzzle is we have to find our way to the 27th room, you naturally think that navigation and wayfinding is important.
[u2233 | 02:58:43.680-02:58:49.560 | Txarro] But in fact, the puzzle is something different, which I actually haven't quite figured out yet.
[u2234 | 02:58:50.790-02:58:57.359 | Txarro] But… I think it's… I think you have to telegraph it in advance that this isn't a…
[u2235 | 02:58:57.780-02:59:04.079 | Txarro] This isn't a way like the, the game experience isn't wayfinding, right?
[u2236 | 02:59:04.080-02:59:04.700 | DM] Right.
[u2237 | 02:59:05.050-02:59:17.299 | Txarro] But in fact, the sort of red herring is that it's kind of set up as if it… because it was called the 27th Room, like, the natural expectation is that this should be a wayfinding
[u2238 | 02:59:18.050-02:59:25.010 | Txarro] And so figuring out how to navigate the rooms was immediately what all of us thought. I mean, it wasn't just me, right? All of us.
[u2239 | 02:59:25.010-02:59:25.460 | DM] Sure, Tim.
[u2240 | 02:59:25.460-02:59:26.010 | Txarro] Thank you.
[u2241 | 02:59:26.130-02:59:27.560 | Txarro] But initially…
[u2242 | 02:59:28.100-02:59:33.240 | Tarek] It turns out the thing about 27 is a clue in itself, because that's like… 3.
[u2243 | 02:59:33.580-02:59:35.170 | Tarek] 3 cubed.
[u2244 | 02:59:35.170-02:59:46.859 | Txarro] Yeah, well, sure, except that getting to the 27th room didn't actually matter. So it wasn't, I mean, yes, there are 27 rooms. It's useful to know that there's 27 rooms.
[u2245 | 02:59:46.860-02:59:47.390 | Edric] Thank you.
[u2246 | 02:59:47.390-02:59:58.370 | Txarro] But that's, I think that's the, like once you've gotten yourself in this situation where you realize that you've set up, you've convinced your players to solve the puzzle that you didn't want them to solve.
[u2247 | 02:59:58.370-02:59:58.920 | DM] Yeah, yeah.
[u2248 | 02:59:58.920-03:00:00.759 | Txarro] Probably just saying.
[u2249 | 03:00:01.330-03:00:09.010 | Txarro] like, above table, This isn't about wayfinding is your best solution.
[u2250 | 03:00:09.010-03:00:20.039 | DM] Got it. Like at that point, it's trying to be subtle about it is not the right thing. It's just to say, Hey, I see you chasing the wrong thing and it's gonna be frustrating. Like, let's not chase the wrong thing.
[u2251 | 03:00:20.400-03:00:20.850 | Tarek] Mmm.
[u2252 | 03:00:20.850-03:00:25.479 | Txarro] Yeah, I think it's better to just redirect, but stepping back, it's even better to…
[u2253 | 03:00:27.080-03:00:32.970 | Txarro] to telegraph what you want the players to do, like, red herrings don't really work in D&D.
[u2254 | 03:00:33.350-03:00:34.690 | Tarek] Mmm.
[u2255 | 03:00:35.050-03:00:42.590 | Txarro] They occasionally work, but they require a deft touch. They don't work in one shots. The only like really good red herring I've ever pulled off was.
[u2256 | 03:00:43.300-03:00:49.200 | Txarro] was… Agata hiding in the ring for a year and a half.
[u2257 | 03:00:49.620-03:00:51.079 | Tarek] But that.
[u2258 | 03:00:51.080-03:00:53.550 | Txarro] It only worked because it was a side quest.
[u2259 | 03:00:53.810-03:00:55.560 | DM] Yeah, yeah.
[u2260 | 03:00:56.060-03:00:56.620 | Edric] And, and.
[u2261 | 03:00:56.620-03:00:57.700 | Txarro] Right? It's like…
[u2262 | 03:00:57.910-03:00:59.590 | Edric] Right. Right.
[u2263 | 03:00:59.590-03:01:01.330 | DM] Set up without room numbers.
[u2264 | 03:01:01.330-03:01:07.679 | Edric] I tried to make it pretty quickly obvious that it was gonna have an impossible, it had an impossible physics.
[u2265 | 03:01:08.190-03:01:09.760 | Edric] I don't know if I succeeded
[u2266 | 03:01:09.980-03:01:16.500 | Edric] But my intention was to say very — I knew you were mapping it, and I tried to introduce very quickly a —.
[u2267 | 03:01:16.500-03:01:24.890 | Txarro] Yeah, but you made some subtle comment like, ha ha, this is going to be interesting. And that was like a clue in and of itself.
[u2268 | 03:01:25.140-03:01:26.320 | Edric] Right, right.
[u2269 | 03:01:26.320-03:01:39.909 | Txarro] But also, like, I think there, like, again, both of these events, like, it's… I mean, I love the vibe of… I love the vibe of both places a huge amount, but the actual gameplay is a little bit, like…
[u2270 | 03:01:40.350-03:01:45.310 | Txarro] There's this desire to make these space-based challenges where the space doesn't matter.
[u2271 | 03:01:46.620-03:01:47.030 | Edric] Right.
[u2272 | 03:01:47.030-03:01:47.550 | Tarek] Oh.
[u2273 | 03:01:47.550-03:01:58.919 | Txarro] And I've realized as a player, I find that kind of frustrating. Like if we're going to be in a space based adventure, the space should matter. Like we should we should have some way of picking which direction is the.
[u2274 | 03:01:59.140-03:02:00.570 | Txarro] Way to go, that's not.
[u2275 | 03:02:00.570-03:02:08.620 | Edric] The space doesn't necessarily provide vibes. A space to purely provide vibes isn't necessarily satisfying to you.
[u2276 | 03:02:08.620-03:02:09.200 | DM] Thank you.
[u2277 | 03:02:09.530-03:02:10.930 | Edric] I… Well, they're all…
[u2278 | 03:02:10.930-03:02:14.929 | Txarro] There's no clues about, there's no clues about, we have no.
[u2279 | 03:02:15.400-03:02:18.960 | Txarro] rational… we have no way to choose which way to go.
[u2280 | 03:02:19.260-03:02:20.759 | Txarro] Which makes it sort of…
[u2281 | 03:02:21.320-03:02:25.099 | Txarro] Like, we always end up in this situation where it's just like, okay, pick a room.
[u2282 | 03:02:26.270-03:02:27.390 | Tarek] Just my impression.
[u2283 | 03:02:27.390-03:02:28.520 | Txarro] Works.
[u2284 | 03:02:29.020-03:02:29.700 | Tarek] My impression of.
[u2285 | 03:02:29.700-03:02:30.190 | Txarro] Sorry.
[u2286 | 03:02:30.190-03:02:36.230 | Tarek] This approach is one of… Like a kind of immersion in a world that.
[u2287 | 03:02:37.290-03:02:39.629 | Tarek] Moves you around.
[u2288 | 03:02:40.790-03:02:45.360 | Tarek] More than you have, really, agency all the time. Like, we can do things.
